金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 11 月 16 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 黃仁勳請辭衝擊輝達，AI 需求韌性支撐科技股反彈：輝達執行長黃仁勳因涉及國安疑慮的相
關爭議，11/12 正式辭任執行長及董事職務，由財務長 Colette Kress 暫代，輝達當日股價盤
中一度重挫近 12%，惟隨後跌幅收斂收跌 4.3%。領導層更迭引發市場對公司策略及 AI 產業
發展的疑慮，但 CoreWeave 第三季營收年增 168%，因受惠 AI 算力需求強勁，此一利多顯
示 AI 需求尚未顯著轉弱，惟輝達管理團隊穩定性、後續財報及政策調查進展，仍是影響科技

股評價的重要變數。
◼ 美國 10 月通膨高於預期，Fed 升息預期推升美債殖利率：美國 11/10 公布 10 月 CPI 月增
0.35%，高於預期的 0.3%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，亦高於預期的 0.2%，顯示通膨壓力仍具黏
性。因 2025 年政府停擺造成資料缺漏，本次未公布年增率。Fed 理事 Waller 表示，若核心
通膨維持 0.3%左右，12 月應考慮升息 2 碼，市場因此提高對後續緊縮的預期。
◼ 伊朗上週末再度對美軍設施進行攻擊，惟美方尚無反擊行動：美國國務卿魯比歐 11/13 表示，
美國對伊朗造成的經濟壓力已大到沒有必要採取軍事行動。不過，伊朗革命衛隊上週末以無人
機攻擊美軍在巴林的雷達站，但美軍尚無進一步反擊，留意之後美方是否仍以封鎖取代攻擊。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短期審慎、中期仍正向，AI 供應鏈基本面持續具支撐：黃仁勳辭任使輝達面臨管理層
接班、企業治理及政策調查等不確定性，加上 Fed 升息預期及 AI 資料中心監管疑慮，市場
短線波動可能延續。然而，該事件並未直接改變 AI 算力需求，且上週 CoreWeave Q3 財報

營收年增 168%、台積電 10 月營收年增 59.7%，均顯示 AI 基礎建設投資仍具動能。整體對
科技股維持短期審慎、但中長期基本面仍在，台灣及日本等具技術競爭優勢、獲利與現金流
穩健的半導體及 AI 供應鏈亦值得留意。惟市場焦點已由資本支出規模轉向投資報酬與現金
流，後續仍須關注輝達財報及管理團隊能否維持客戶信心。
◼ Fed 升息預期壓抑債市，中天期美債及投資級債仍相對具吸引力： 美國 10 月 CPI 高於預
期，加上 Fed 官員偏鷹表態，使債券市場持續承受政策利率上修壓力。美國 10 年期公債殖
利率重回 5%以上，收益水準已具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券雖然價格波動較小，但殖
利率較低，且未來仍面臨政策利率及再投資風險；超長天期債券則易受到財政赤字、發債供
給與期限溢價影響。中天期美債在收益與利率風險之間相對均衡，維持審慎正向看法。信用
債方面，投資級債收益條件仍具支撐，惟須留意高資本支出企業的融資與現金流風險。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額為 7,450 億美元，月增 0.8%、年增 6.3%；
扣除汽車及零件銷售後月增 0.9%，進一步扣除汽車、零件及加油站後
之核心零售銷售月增 0.2%。加油站、餐飲及非實體零售銷售持續成長，
顯示美國消費需求仍具韌性，惟成長動能較前月略有放緩。 
⚫ 2026 財年美國聯邦政府財政赤字達 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，年增 12.2%，
主因政府支出成長高於收入。9 月單月由去年同期盈餘轉為 269 億美元
赤字，關稅退稅增加壓縮收入，而全年淨利息支出突破 1 兆美元、年增
8.4%。在聯邦債務總額逾 40 兆美元下，財政赤字擴大及債務融資成本
攀升，持續考驗財政永續性。 
⚫ 9 月美國成屋銷售年率 399 萬戶，月增 1.5%、年減 2.2%；成屋中位數
售價 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，連續 39 個月上漲。待售庫存增至 160
萬戶、供給量 4.8 個月，顯示供給改善，惟高利率環境仍抑制購屋需求。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於 8 月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率由 2.4%
升至 2.6%。受能源價格上揚影響，能源指數月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格
大幅上漲，成為推升通膨之主要因素；住房指數續增 0.4%。整體而言，
美國通膨壓力較前月升溫，核心物價漲勢亦略有增強。 
⚫ 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 1.3%，年增率由 5.6%升至 6.3%；能
源價格大漲 10.8%為主要推升因素，帶動商品價格月增 2.6%。服務價格
亦月增 0.8%，核心 PPI 年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%，顯示生產端通膨壓力
持續升高。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月中旬，多位聯準會官員表示通膨仍具壓力。Mary Daly 認為未來可
能仍需升息，但 10 月底會議無迫切行動必要；Beth Hammack 警告若通
膨預期惡化，可能加大緊縮力道；主席 Kevin Warsh 則指出美國經濟仍
具韌性，但能源價格上漲及 AI 投資擴張恐推升通膨壓力。整體而言，
聯準會立場偏鷹，但短期升息時點仍視通膨走勢而定。 
⚫ 美墨關稅談判原接近達成初步協議，惟美國商務部長反對調降汽車及鋼
鋁關稅，使協商進展受阻。墨西哥則爭取降低汽車關稅及擴大零組件抵

等方式因應配額限制，藉此維持在歐洲市場的競爭力與市占率。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.19 
一、 美國—內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
2026 年 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,450 億美元，較
上月增加 0.8%，年增 6.3%。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.9%；進一步
扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增 0.2%，顯示整體消費需求仍維持
成長。從主要業別表現觀察，加油站、非實體零售商及餐飲服務與飲酒場所銷售
額均較上月增加，其中加油站及餐飲消費表現相對亮眼，電子及家電商店銷售亦
呈成長。整體而言，9 月美國零售銷售延續擴張走勢，顯示民間消費仍具支撐力
道，且多數主要消費類別維持正成長，惟核心零售增幅較前月放緩，後續仍須關
注物價、就業及金融情勢變化對消費支出的影響。 
2026 年 9 月(2026 財年第 12 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字為 269.0 億美元，較
去年同期 1,979.4 億美元財政盈餘轉為赤字。當月政府收入 5,578.8 億美元，年增
2.6%；政府支出 5,847.8 億美元，年增 69.1%，支出增幅明顯高於收入。收入方
面，9 月關稅淨收入由 8 月的 128.4 億美元降至 59.1 億美元，累計 2026 財年關稅
淨收入約 1,732.2 億美元，年減 11.1%，關稅退稅增加已部分抵銷關稅收入成長。
支出方面，9 月聯邦政府淨利息支出約 347.9 億美元，全年累計達 1 兆 517.5 億美
元，年增 8.4%，反映高利率環境下債務利息負擔持續攀升。累計 2026 財年全年，
美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，較 2025 財年擴大 12.2%。其中，
政府收入年增 3.2%至 5 兆 4,033.3 億美元，支出年增 5.5%至 7 兆 3,958.0 億美元，
顯示支出成長仍快於收入增加。從支出結構觀察，社會安全、醫療保險、國防及
淨利息支出均較上年增加，持續推升財政壓力。整體而言，美國財政赤字擴大主
要反映政府支出成長強勁、利息負擔居高不下，以及關稅退稅增加削弱部分收入
效果。在聯邦債務總額突破 40 兆美元背景下，未來財政赤字走勢、債務融資成本
及財政永續性問題，仍將是市場關注的重要焦點。 
2026 年 9 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率升至 399 萬戶，較上月增加 1.5%，
惟較去年同期減少 2.2%，整體房市成交動能仍偏疲弱。區域表現方面，南部及西
部分別月增 2.8%，中西部持平，東北部月減 2.1%，四大地區銷售量均較去年同
期下滑。房價方面，成屋售價中位數為 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，已連續 39 個

月維持年增走勢。供給端則持續增加，待售成屋庫存升至 160 萬戶，較上月增加
1.9%，較去年同期增加 4.6%，相當於 4.8 個月供給量，高於去年同期的 4.5 個月。
全美住房可負擔指數由去年同期 106.7 降至 105.4，顯示購屋負擔仍受房價及利率
影響。整體而言，9 月美國成屋市場呈現成交量略有回升、房價維持溫和漲勢及
待售庫存持續累積的格局，顯示供給條件改善，但高利率環境仍對購屋需求形成
壓力。 
2026 年 9 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於上月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；扣除食品與能源之核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，與上月
持平，年增率則由 2.4%升至 2.6%。在細項方面，能源價格持續上揚，能源指數
月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格大幅上漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅逾半，為本月通
膨升溫主因；住房指數月增 0.4%，住房項目價格漲幅尚未明顯趨緩。食品指數則
持平，其中外食價格月增 0.2%，家庭食品價格月減 0.1%。此外，二手車及卡車、
機票、休閒娛樂及服飾等價格上漲，通訊價格則下跌。整體而言，9 月美國通膨
明顯回升，主要受汽油價格大幅上漲及住房成本續增帶動；核心 CPI 月增率維持
0.3%，年增率則回升至 2.6%，顯示核心物價壓力較前月略有增強，未來通膨走勢
仍須持續關注能源價格變化及住房等服務項目價格表現。 
 2026 年 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 1.3%，高於上月之
0.5%，年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 2.6%，主要受
能源價格月增 10.8%帶動，以汽油、柴油及航空燃油價格漲幅較為顯著；食品價
格亦月增 1.2%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.8%，主要反映批發及零售
利潤率、運輸服務價格上升。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求 PPI
月增 0.5%，高於上月之 0.3%，年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%。整體而言，9 月 PPI 升
幅明顯擴大，主要反映能源與服務價格同步上揚，顯示生產端成本壓力仍偏高，
後續仍須關注能源價格及運輸成本變化對通膨走勢之影響。 
10 月中旬，多位聯準會官員針對貨幣政策發表看法。舊金山聯準銀行總裁戴
利(Mary Daly)表示，雖然通膨仍高於 2%目標，未來可能仍需進一步升息，但目
前無迫切必要在 10 月 27 日至 28 日 FOMC 會議立即採取行動，顯示聯準會傾向
先觀察後續數據表現。克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth Hammack)則警告，若
通膨預期持續惡化，聯準會可能必須採取更積極的緊縮措施，以維持物價穩定。

投資熱潮支撐而維持韌性，但能源價格上漲及 AI 投資擴張恐推升通膨壓力，並

近期美墨貿易談判再度受到關稅爭議影響。據《華爾街日報》報導，雙方原
已接近達成初步協議，美國擬調降對墨西哥汽車、鋼鐵及鋁製品的部分進口關稅，
以換取墨西哥在貿易政策、商品採購及其他經貿議題上的讓步，並為後續《美墨
加協定》（USMCA）檢討與延長協商創造有利條件。然而，美國商務部長 Howard 
Lutnick 反對大幅調降目前對墨西哥汽車課徵的 25%關稅，以及鋼鐵、鋁製品與部
分衍生製成品的 50%關稅，使談判進展受阻。墨西哥方面則希望將汽車關稅降至
15%或以下，以縮小與日本、南韓汽車適用稅率的差距，並擴大汽車關稅抵減機
制對美墨加三國零組件價值的認列範圍；惟美方仍須考量國內金屬產業對降稅可
能衝擊本土生產及就業的疑慮。儘管雙方對關稅調整幅度仍存分歧，墨西哥經貿
官員表示協商已有部分進展，美國商務部亦強調持續與墨方保持溝通，顯示談判
尚未正式破局。整體而言，美墨關稅協商結果不僅攸關北美汽車及金屬產業供應
鏈成本與競爭力，也可能影響 USMCA 後續檢討與延長協商進程；若雙方遲遲無
法縮小分歧，恐增加企業投資與供應鏈布局的不確定性，並對北美區域貿易整合
形成壓力。 
二、歐元區—工業生產走疲，前景有待觀察 
 歐元區 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)較 7 月下滑 0.5%，呈現連續三個月下降，
其中非耐久消費財、中間財及能源產出分別減少 2.7%、0.8%、0.6%，僅資本財、
耐久消費財產出分別成長 0.4%、0.2%。儘管德國 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)反彈
0.4%，係受惠車廠陸續結束歲修，但法、義、西工業生產分別下跌 0.3%、1.3%、
0.8%為主要拖累因素，反映先前美伊戰爭帶來的庫存儲備需求逐漸消退，尤其以
能源密集產業(化工、造紙、鋼鐵等)變化最為明顯。整體而言，即便排除能源密集
產業的波動，其他製造業諸如製藥、汽車、機械等生產情形皆偏疲軟。

三、日本—AI 投資續強，帶動機械訂單穩健復甦 
 8 月核心機械訂單(扣除波動訂單)月增 5.2%，其中製造業訂單月增 1.6%，人

資為主要支撐。 
四、中國—AI 相關需求帶動出口動能強勁成長 
9 月出口年增率較 8 月大幅增加 11.3 個百分點至 36.3%，出口總值為 4,472
億美元；累計前九個月出口年增率上升為 21.3%，累積出口總值為 33,707 億美元。
前九個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(108.1%)、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等，54.6%)、船舶(29.9%)、汽車(54.3%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備
出口加速成長，為 9 月出口年增率大幅上升的主要因素。 
9 月進口年增率較 8 月增 2.8 個百分點至 31%，進口總值為 3,137 億美元；累
計前九個月進口值成長 27.5%至 24,326 億美元。其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等)進口分別成長 63.5%、124.8%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部
分伺服器關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前九個月貿易順差達
9,381 億美元，僅較去年同期增加 7.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時
伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價
值產品依賴國際供應鏈支持。 
9 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 8 月的 3.8%升至 5%，主因原油價格自 7 月起走
升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年增率
續揚 14.1 個百分點至 24.5%，並帶動燃料加工業出廠價格年增率上揚至 21.6%，
此外，對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學品、化纖出廠價格年增率加速升至
12.1%、12.3%，顯示原油價格上漲的影響開始擴散至中下游產業。此外，由於近
期鋼鐵價格走弱，黑色金屬礦產開採、冶煉加工業出廠價格分別年減 3.4%、0.2%，

展望未來，歐中談判已就汽車貿易達成部分共識，包含未來中國出口至歐盟
的混合動力汽車設定每年 35 萬輛的配額上限，且配額規模可逐年增加 6%。依照
歐盟預估 2026 年自中國進口約 80 萬輛混合動力汽車推算，中國對歐盟的混合動
力車出口量將較原先潛在規模減少一半以上。雖該協議不利於汽車出口，但有助
於避免歐盟進一步擴大貿易保護措施，中國車廠則可能會加速透過赴歐投資設
廠、提升當地生產比重等方式因應配額限制，藉此維持在歐洲市場的競爭力與市
占率。

新預估 2026 年經濟成長達 5.2%、2027 年放緩至 4.6%。 
六、台灣—AI 帶動出口、投資與消費成長，中經院上修 2026 年經濟預測 
中經院 10 月 16 日公布最新預測，上修 2026 年台灣經濟成長率至 12.16%，
創 1988 年以來新高，主要受惠於 AI 技術快速發展帶動全球科技投資，半導體與
資通訊產品需求強勁，出口維持高速成長，同時民間投資與消費亦同步擴張，經
濟成長動能由內、外需共同支撐。另一方面，由於中東地緣政治風險升溫帶動油
價維持高檔，能源價格外溢效果推升整體物價，中經院將 2026 年 CPI 年增率預
估上修至 2.06%，惟整體通膨仍屬相對溫和。

Document 066: 2026年10月进出口数据点评：进出口同比延续高位增长，贸易顺差有所回落

-2026.11.2  
进出口同比延续高位增长，贸易顺差有所回落 
——2026年 10月进出口数据点评

陈曦（分析师） 王帅中（联系人）  
chenxi2@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790521100002 
wangshuaizhong@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790125070016

事件：海关总署公布 2026年 10月进出口商品统计快讯，按美元计价，我国进口
同比+42.1%（前值为+31.2%，下同），环比-2.1%(+11.1%)；出口同比+35.2%
（+36.3%），环比-7.8%（+11.4%）；贸易顺差同比+18.5%（+49.7%），环比-21.2%
（+12.1%），进出口同比保持高位增长，环比增速均有所下降。 
⚫ 10月进出口数据关注点 
10月进出口同比均延续高增，增长速度且均达 30%以上，同比增长均远超市场
预期，受国庆假期影响，环比均较前值有所下降，进口增速高于出口增速，贸
易顺差有所收窄。出口同比+35.2%（+36.3%），进口同比+42.1%（+31.2%），进
出口金额均持续处于历史高位，进口同比 2026年以来均保持两位数增长，出口
同比增速连续两月保持 30%以上增速。Wind统计机构预测当月出口同比中位数
为+32.5%，平均数为+32.2%；进口同比中位数为+30.9%，平均数为 29.9%，10
月进出口同比均超市场预期，进出口 2026年以来始终保持高景气度，一方面是
由于全球 AI投资扩张，AI链上商品进出口高增拉动，另一方面国内“内卷”与
科技进步的共同推动下，中国商品的高性价比推动中国出口持续超预期增长。进
口超预期或主要与国际油价中枢抬升带动原油、成品油同比增速提升相关，出口
超预期与全球经济保持高景气有关，10月全球制造业 PMI为 52.6，保持扩张区
间。 
AI投资扩张仍是进出口高涨的最重要因素，价格上涨在其中起到的作用也不容
忽视。AI链上产品出口高增拉动出口增长，集成电路出口持续加速，10月出口
同比增长 137.6%。农产品、化工医药、劳动密集型商品等非 AI链上商品出口同
比均保持增长，分别增长 5.9%、34.0%和 17.5%，或主要源于 2025年同期基数
较低，且价格上涨起到的作用不容忽视，国际油价中枢持续攀升，价格上涨进一
步推动出口金额高增，但同比增长也一定程度上反应非 AI链条上商品出口的改
善。进口同比增速达 40%以上，AI产业链发展仍是最大助力，原材料价格上涨
一定程度上也起到助推作用。自动数据处理设备及其零部件 10月进口同比增长
261.7%，集成电路同比增长 78.5%。 
从出口的产品结构来看，出口产品保持分化态势，程度上有所收窄。AI相关产
业链发展推动集成电路、高新技术产品出口增速高增，带动整体增速提升，劳动
密集型商品出口持续修复。10月集成电路当月出口同比增长 137.6%，高新技术
产品同比增长 66.8%，均保持高速增长。劳动密集型商品 9月同比增长 22.8%，
10月同比增长 17.5%，依旧保持两位数增长，2025年基数的下降对修复带来一
定的支撑。我国货物贸易不断提质升级，出口由劳动密集型产品向高质、高技术
产品转变。 
从出口国家来看，10月我国对主要国家与地区的进出口基本均保持同比增长，
出口多元化是我国出口增速持续增长的动能所在。10 月对美国进出口同比均保
持高增，一方面与 2025年同期基数较低有关，也与科技公司资本开支增长下对
AI链上产品需求高增相关。从港口高频数据来看，10月重点港口完成集装箱吞
吐月度周均值同比增长 8.2%，高频数据与实际出口情况相符，一定程度上已提
前指明本次出口超预期增长。需要注意的是，11月、12月 2025年同期基数有所
抬升，或会对后续出口增速产生影响，但在全球经济保持高景气度的背景下，四
季度出口增速整体或仍具韧性。  
⚫ 债市观点：债券收益率或将上行，警惕债市的反转风险 
对于债市，我们认为债券收益率或将上行，警惕债市的反转风险。 
第一，市场大多观点认为，后续 DR001将围绕 1.25%运行，这存在落空的可能
性，或有利多出尽的风险。第二，金融时报连发两文指出“央行研究切换公开市
场操作主力品种，属于货币政策框架改革的一项举措，不同于通常讲的货币政策
降息加息…市场各方要避免将央行操作机制改革举措当作降息来炒作”，或指明
监管对当前债市的态度趋于谨慎。第三，拥挤度层面，当前公募等非银机构债券
久期已达近年高位，特别是经过这轮换锚发酵后，拥挤度再次提升，当前存在拥
挤度调整、估值回调风险。第四，基本面层面，从 9月经济数据来看，国内经济
有触底回升迹象，9月社融同比 2025年 9月正增，政府债发行速度加快，政策
逐渐落地下经济动能边际修复，当前债市的定价尚未反应基本面的边际修复，这
也是潜在的风险。 
⚫ 风险提示：政策变化超预期；经济变化超预期。

投资者类别仅限定为专业投资者及风险承受能力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者。若您并非专业投资者及风险承受能
力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者，请取消阅读，请勿收藏、接收或使用本研报中的任何信息。

股票投资评级说明
评级 说明
证券评级
买入（Buy） 预计相对强于市场表现 20%以上；
增持（outperform） 预计相对强于市场表现 5%～20%；
中性（Neutral） 预计相对市场表现在－5%～＋5%之间波动；
减持（underperform） 预计相对弱于市场表现 5%以下。
行业评级
看好（overweight）  预计行业超越整体市场表现；
中性（Neutral） 预计行业与整体市场表现基本持平；
看淡（underperform） 预计行业弱于整体市场表现。
备注：评级标准为以报告日后的 6~12个月内，证券相对于市场基准指数的涨跌幅表现，其中 A股基准指数为沪
深 300指数、港股基准指数为恒生指数、新三板基准指数为三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的）、美股基准指数为标普 500或纳斯达克综合指数。我们在此提醒您，不同证券研究机构采用不同
的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决

定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较

### Page 6

Quanta Computer (2382.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Quanta Computer
Company description
Founded in 1988, Quanta Computer is a Taiwan-based original design
manufacturer (ODM) of notebook (NB) PCs and other electronic hardware. It
is the world's largest NB ODM in terms of shipments, with major customers
including the leading Taiwan, US, and Japanese electronics brands. In
addition to notebooks, Quanta is expanding into cloud-related product
development, which accounted for 30-35% of total revenues in 2022.

Investment strategy
We rate Quanta at Buy. We expect stabilizing NB shipment, solid server sales
growth amid increasing AI-related applications, as well as rising EV
component exposure to drive better margins and stronger earnings growth.
The company’s attractive cash yield should also help support the shares on
the downside in case of market volatility.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$450 is based on a 14x PE multiple on 2027E earnings.
The target multiple is in line with historical trading average since 2021, mainly

to reflect strong earnings growth trajectory brought about by servers/AI
servers and EV components. We use a P/E-based methodology as we believe
the market will be primarily focused on Quanta's earnings performance given
the dynamics of the NB/server industry.
Risks
Downside risks that could prevent the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) slower-than-expected server/AI server demand growth; 2)
execution risks relating to margin improvement such as production yield rate

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Accton’s 3Q26 results, released on 12 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat/miss expectations. Accton’s 3Q26 
net profit came in at TWD13.1bn (EPS: TWD23.3), in line with our estimate 
(TWD13.0bn) but 6% lower than the Bloomberg consensus (TWD13.9bn).  
Revenue was TWD120.7bn (+26% QoQ, 66% YoY), higher than our/BBG 
estimates by 13%/2%, mainly due to increasing AI card contribution for 
Trainium 3. Nonetheless, gross margin fell by 2.1pp QoQ to 17.6% (vs. our 
19.1% estimate), reflecting rising material costs (especially from memory). 
Consequently, OPM declined by 1.6pp to 13.4% (vs. our 15.1% estimate), 
which was slightly offset by better operating leverage.

Business outlook. We believe gross margin is likely the key highlight of 
the 3Q26 results. Per management, gross margin was diluted due to rising 
memory costs. As such, Accton has shifted its business model from “Buy-&-
Sell” to consignment from September. Since then, we see gross margin 
gradually recovering back to the 20% level. Looking into 4Q26, we believe 
Accton could sustain the gross margin, but the networking chipset shortage 
might weigh on revenue momentum in the short term. The alternative is 
that one of the key switch clients could shift its orders slightly towards 
Spectrum first to ease the near-term shortage issue, in our view. As for the 
AI card business, we believe the total shipment has been largely stable 
since 3Q26. Even when the end-client gradually shifts its product mix from 
air-cooled to the liquid-cooled version, we believe the rising content from 
the liquid-cooled version should be able to offset the slightly lower ASP per 
chipset given there are more chipsets (4x vs. 2x from air-cooled) on an 
ASIC AI motherboard. Looking into 2027, we maintain our original view that 
total AMZN ASIC units will grow c.30% YoY to c.3m as the first growth 
drivers. On top of this, the liquid-cooled version has adopted the PCIE 
scale-up switch, which can expand TAM for Accton, in our view. Lastly, for 
the top-of-rack (ToR) switches, we expect 800G switches to remain the 
mainstream models in 2027 but with rising enterprise usage, which could 
likely be the next growth driver in 2027.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 6-12% to factor in 
higher ToR switch revenue. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, but lower our 
12M TP to TWD3,000 (prior: TWD3,666), based on a target PER multiple of 
24x (prior: 35x, within its past-3-year range of 10-33x), applied to our 1-
year forward EPS forecast, to reflect the weaker market sentiment. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected ASIC server and switch demand.

How we differ: Our 2027E EPS is 5% higher than the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely due to our more positive view on operating leverage.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: gross margin dilution on memory costs

➢ Fundamentals largely on track with our prior estimates  
➢ Recent pullbacks provide good entry points to build positions 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1988, Accton is dedicated to the design and manufacturing of enterprise networking and 
communication products. With its long-term experience and expertise, Accton is one of the leading 
open-source networking ODM partners for major players worldwide, with particular focuses on data 
centres, telco carriers and campus networks. Major products include enterprise switches, metro access 
switches, network applications and wireless products.

### Page 5

5

Accton Technology (2345 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Accton’s board has 7 directors, with 3 independent directors. The independent directors account
for 43% of all directors. A total of 8 board meetings were held in 2025, and the average actual
attendance rate was 95%.
The backgrounds of independent directors are diversified and their competence includes
business management, accounting, finance, and work experience in the medical industry. We
deem the diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality.
Besides, the Chairman and President are not the same person, which increases the board and
executive management’s quality. Based on its board structure and key executive hiring, we
believe that Accton has already professionalised the family business by allowing external
professionals to run the business for major shareholders.
Capital management 1
Accton declared DPS of TWD15.0 in 2025, representing a 31.8% payout ratio. The ratio was
lower than the 51.2% in 2024 and its historical range of 69-75% from 2018 to 2022. That said, it
still points towards a friendly shareholder dividend policy, in our view.
We attribute the lower payout ratio mainly to the sharp earnings growth and continued capacity
investments in Taiwan and Vietnam amid US-China tensions. The DPS CAGR in the past 10
years was c.23%, which showcased Accton’s capability in capital management. The continuous
improvement in networking capabilities (eg, 400G and 800G switches), alongside expansion into
AI accelerator modules, has helped it differentiate itself from other competitors and strengthen its
position in the white-box switch supply chain for major US cloud service providers.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.15% of total revenue, while purchases
from related parties accounted for 2.0% of cost of sales. We see limited risk from related-party
transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
Like most vendors, Accton asks its suppliers to follow: 1) the hazard substance requirements of
the RoHS, Reach and corporate social responsibilities (5 categories, including labour, health and
safety, environmental, ethics and management system) required by the RBA Code of Conduct,
and 2) QC08000 (Hazardous Substances Process Management), while the company conducts
annual reviews to evaluate its suppliers’ performance.
To effectively track, communicate and manage its supply chain to manufacture green products,
each supplier can use the eGreen system® platform, developed independently by Accton, to
learn about environmental protection control and management requirements for components and
materials.
S Data security 2
To ensure information security for customers and the company, Accton obtained ISO/IEC
27001:2013 certification in 2016 and completed the transition to ISO/IEC 27001:2022 in 2024,
with continued verification of its information security policies and controls in 2025. Apart from the
global information security standards, Accton also takes measures to improve its information
security, including:1) Implementing business continuity and disaster recovery mechanisms,
including annual recovery drills for critical systems and databases. In 2025, the company
achieved its target of recovering critical systems within 48 hours. 2) Conducting monthly
vulnerability scans across company-wide servers, network equipment and core systems to
identify potential security risks. Accton also conducts quarterly social engineering drills to
strengthen employee awareness of cybersecurity threats.
In 2025, Accton conducted four phishing simulation exercises involving over 13,000 participant
attendances, achieving an average phishing safe rate of 93%. The company reported no major
information security incidents or personal data breaches during the year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on preliminary JMTBA orders, which we
believe could be a catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
According to the Japan Machine Tool Builders' Association (JMTBA), headline

orders dropped 8% MoM but grew 43% YoY in October  2026 (vs. +13% MoM/
+60% YoY in September 2026), to ¥204bn.
• Domestic orders were -18% MoM/+47% YoY (vs. +24% MoM/+46% YoY in
September), at ¥52bn.
• Overseas orders were -5% MoM/+41% YoY (vs. +9% MoM/+67% YoY  in
September), at ¥152bn.
Our view:
• For overseas orders, the growth remains solid on a YoY basis. We think the
sequential decline is likely from China as its manufacturing PMI edged down

to 49.3 in Oct (vs. 50.1 in Sep). Meanwhile, North America and Europe could
prove more resilient, with manufacturing PMIs staying relatively firm at
55.5/52.7 in Oct (vs. 54.5/52.9 in Sep)
• We remain OW on both Airtac (1590.TW) and Hiwin (2049.TW), as we see
company-specific drivers on top of the industry recovery trend:
° Airtac should continue to benefit from its own market share gains.
Valuation also appears undemanding at 15x 2027e P/E (vs. 25x average

since 2020).
° Hiwin should  see margin expansion in the coming quarters, driven by
higher utilization and price hikes. Current valuation of 27x 2027 P/E
implies upside vs. peak cycle valuation at 35-40x P/E.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 25x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). We think our valuation of 25x P/E is reasonable (vs. average of 25x since
2020).
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China
n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation

Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 34x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 50% operating profit

CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup
n Market share gains
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand
n More pricing pressure
n Market share loss
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

 절대 (6.6) (10.0) (6.5)

 절대 (달러환산) (5.2) (2.7) 0.2

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

유틸리티업종

◼ 목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적, 소규모 SMR 수주 등은 긍정적 
◼ 구체적인 미국 원전 사업개발 시작, 2H27 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
◼ 2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조

목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적 양호, 소규모 SMR 수주는 긍정적 
투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 85,000원으로 하향. 이제는 성장 스토리보다
는 실질적인 원전 수주 확대가 필요한 상황. 의미 있는 수준의 원전 수주 확
대는 2H27부터 본격화될 가능성 존재. 다만, 단기적인 원전 수주 모멘텀 부
족 등을 감안해 6개월 목표주가를 현실화했음 
1) 3Q26 연결기준 영업이익 2,612억원(+91%, 이하 YoY), 에너빌리티 부문 
영업이익 1,211억원(+179%) 등 양호한 실적 기록. 이는 에너빌리티의 가스
터빈, 원전 기자재 매출 증가 등에 기인, 2) 10월 29일 NuScale과 1개 모듈 
주기기 공급 계약을 체결했으며, 4Q26 X-energy와 4개 모듈의 일부 장납기 
품목/소재 수주가 예상되는 등 2Q26 ~ 3Q26 TerraPower와의 일부 품목 계
약에 이어 개별 건의 규모는 작지만 다수의 SMR 관련 기자재 수주가 늘어나
는 점은 긍정적. 3) 최근 폴란드 AP1000 3기의 선발주 장납기 품목 확정(폴
란드 공급업체의 대상 생산 품목 확정, 원자로 압력용기 및 증기발생기는 미
확정)되었으며, 미국 원전 프로젝트가 구체화되기 시작. 4) 향후 AP1000향 
공급 기자재 품목 확대 가능성도 존재. 이는 2H27 본격적인 원전 수주금액 
확대로 이어질 것으로 기대

미국 원전은 구체적 사업개발 시작, 2H27 이후 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
2026년 6월 DOE의 ‘원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 발표(AP1000 10기)’, 8월 
TVA의 ‘2026 IRP’에 2040년까지 최대 원전 5.4GW 승인, 9월 한국과 미국 
상무부 간의 원전 투자 8기 MOU 체결 등을 통해 미국 원전 건설 재개 정책 
및 제도 개편이 구체화되고 강화되었음. 미국 원전 시장은 4Q26 ~ 2027년 
구체적인 사업개발 단계를 거쳐(부지 선정, 원전 운영 SPC 설립, 금융계약체
결, COL 신청 등), 2H27 ~ 2028년 장납기품목 선발주, 2028 ~ 2029년 건
설 계약 단계 진입 예상. 동사는 4Q26 미국 및 국내 가스터빈, 국내 공공 해
상풍력 프로젝트용 터빈, 폴란드 AP1000 주기기 등 미국 원전 이외 분야에
서의 수주 확대 예상. 현재 Rolls-Royce 등과도 SMR 주기기 공급 관련 논의 
중이며, NuScale SMR은 2027년 PPA 체결 시, COL 및 FID 이전 SMR 모듈 
11기(4Q26 1기 계약)의 수주가 가능할 것으로 기대

2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조 
대우건설·두산에너빌리티 컨소시엄의 체코 원전 시공 계약 협상은 종료되었
지만, 2027년 7월 신규 시공사와 계약 예정. 동사는 입찰에 다시 참여할 예
정. 이번 이슈의 핵심 쟁점은 이미 체코 측과 공사비총액(Lump-sum) 계약을 
맺은 한수원이 시공사와 총액계약을 맺기를 원한 것임. 이번 이슈로 체코 두
코바니 원전 EPC 프로젝트 수익성에 대한 우려가 발생할 수 있지만, 실제 건
설 과정에서 추가 비용이 공사비 계약금액(예비비 포함)을 초과할지는 예단하
기 어려움. 하도급 관리, 공기 준수 등 시공능력의 중요성이 강조되는 상황. 
동사는 3년 전부터 체코에 시공 인력 상주하며 시공 환경 자료 확보, 초기 인
허가, 설계 업무 관여해 왔으며, 주기기 공급사로서 기자재 특성을 활용해 설
치까지 수행 가능한 경쟁력 등을 바탕으로 성공적 수주를 목표하고 있음

표 4. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업의 대상 장납기품목(LLI; Long-Lead Items) 
 품목 설명  대출과 관련된 확인 근거 
원자로압력용기 
(RPV; Reactor Pressure Vessel) 
핵연료와 노심을 수용하고 원자로 냉각재의 압력을 유지하는 
용기. 앞서 설명한 격납용기와 구분되는 원자로 주기기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
증기발생기 
(SG; Steam Generator) 
원자로에서 나온 열을 전달받아 터빈을 돌릴 증기를 만드는 대
형 열교환기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
사전제작 구조 모듈 
(PSM; Prefabricated Structural Modules) 
원전 건물의 벽체·바닥 등 구조물을 모듈로 미리 제작하는 방
식의 구성품. AP1000에는 강판과 콘크리트로 구성하는 구조 
모듈이 사용됨 
DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23. 다만, 미국 대
출자료에 개별 모듈 코드까지 열거되지는 않음 
원자로냉각재펌프 
(RCP; Reactor Coolant Pump) 
원자로 냉각재를 순환시켜 원자로에서 발생한 열을 증기발생
기로 전달하는 펌프 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
격납용기 
(CV; Containment Vessel) 
원자로압력용기와 주요 설비를 둘러싸며 방사성 물질의 외부 
방출을 억제하는 대형 강철 밀폐 구조물 
DOE EDF 담당자의 직접 발언에서 확인 — 
2026.10.20 공개 인터뷰 
자료: DOE, 대신증권 Research Center

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

Document 257: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

Document 272: AI基建股蒸发4.2万亿美元，“卖铲盛宴”终结？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI基建股蒸发4.2万亿美元，“卖铲盛宴”终结？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)

### Page 1

6 ⽉以来，卡特彼勒跌 44% 、 Oklo 暴跌 49% 。分析师直指根源：投资者将传统⼯业股当成
⻓股炒作，估值严重虚⾼。此次抛售的另⼀重要背景，是美国中期选举后数据中⼼监管政
策⾛向的不明朗。基本⾯仍具韧性 —— 卡特彼勒积压订单达 780 亿美元，多家企业营收增⻓
已锁定⾄ 2027 年。
摘要：
全球制造、公⽤事业与矿业企业⾃今年 6 ⽉以来市值合计蒸发 4.2 万亿美元，这场抛售⼏乎抹去了
此前 12 个⽉内同类企业所积累的 9.4 万亿美元涨幅的近半数，曾⻛靡⼀时的 AI“ 卖铲 ” 交易正在急
速逆转。
据英国《⾦融时报》分析，在媒体报道中与数据中⼼建设关联度越⾼的企业，遭受的冲击越为
惨烈。投资者对 AI 热潮的信⼼正在动摇，从适配超⼤规模数据中⼼的燃⽓发电机组制造商，到连
接 AI 服务器机架所需的⾼端光纤企业，此前被争相追捧的标的如今纷纷遭到抛售。⼯业板块已成
为今夏以来标普 500 指数中表现第⼆差的板块，仅次于科技板块。
市场的转向折射出更⼴泛的担忧：超⼤规模云计算企业持续以债务融资推⾼资本开⽀，叠加美

国国内对数据中⼼建设⽇益⾼涨的政治阻⼒，令投资者开始质疑这场建设热潮能否持续。
⻰头企业市值腰斩
数据中⼼燃⽓发电机组的主要供应商卡特彼勒（ Caterpillar ）过去六个⽉跌幅超过 30% ，⽽⾃ 6
⽉ 30 ⽇股价触及历史⾼点以来，市值已缩⽔ 2140 亿美元，跌幅达 44% 。
此外，核电企业 Oklo 尽管在今年 1 ⽉与 Meta 签署了供电合同，股价仍重挫 49% 。暖通空调系统制
造商 Comfort Systems 与光纤制造商康宁（ Corning ）在同⼀时期均损失了约⼀半市值。
据英国⾦融时报称，德意志银⾏⾼级美国经济学家 Brett Ryan 表⽰，此前有⼤量机构资⾦ “ 盲⽬
涌⼊ ” 这些交易， “ 可能助推了部分企业过于乐观的估值 ”。他还指出，股票市场正在 “ 向信贷市
场早已发出的信号靠拢 ”—— 后者对数据中⼼建设中⻛险融资的增⻓早有警⽰。
⻛险投资⼈、⿇省理⼯学院研究员 Paul Kedrosky 则直⾔，投资者将传统⼯业企业当作半导体股
票来对待，本⾝就是⼀个错误。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

不过，尽管股价承压，多家企业的经营基本⾯依然稳健。美国政府最新数据显⽰，截⾄今年 9 ⽉
的 12 个⽉内，美国数据中⼼建设⽀出（不含 IT 设备与芯⽚）达到 960 亿美元。
第三季度业绩同样印证了需求的韧性。卡特彼勒报告积压订单达创纪录的 780 亿美元，其发电业
务销售额同⽐增⻓ 75% ，主要由数据中⼼需求驱动。西⻔⼦来⾃数据中⼼的电⽓设备订单积压
在 2026 年增⻓ 60% ，达到 70 亿欧元；伊顿（ Eaton ）同类订单则实现同⽐翻倍。多家燃⽓轮机
制造商表⽰， 2026 年前九个⽉已斩获数⼗亿美元订单。
Dell'Oro Group 分析师 Alex Cordovil 表⽰， “ 就这些企业的营收基本⾯和潜在盈利前景⽽⾔，我
们仍然看到⾮常强劲的预期。 ” 他补充道，电⽓化和供应链重构等更宏观的趋势持续⽀撑着众多⼯
业企业的核⼼业务， “ 建设并未减速 …… 积压订单处于历史⾼位，这意味着⾄少到 2027 年，营收
增⻓已基本锁定。 ”
⼯业控制设备制造商 Allient ⾸席执⾏官 Richard S Warzala 在 11 ⽉ 5 ⽇的业绩电话会上表⽰，对 AI

的需求不会下滑。
此外， Kedrosky 对订单积压数据持保留态度，认为这是滞后指标，反映的是此前的预期，⽽⾮
当下的真实需求。他指出，企业存在 " 双重乃⾄三重下单 " 的现象 —— 为了在感知到的供应紧缺中
抢占排队位置⽽超额备货。
Brett Ryan 的判断则更为直接：股票市场正在完成⼀次迟来的估值校正，向信贷市场早已传递的
⻛险信号对⻬。这场历时数⽉、市值蒸发逾 4 万亿美元的调整，本质上是资本市场对 " ⼀切皆成⻓
股 " 叙事的系统性重新定价。
中期选举后的监管不确定性
此次抛售的另⼀重要背景，是美国中期选举后数据中⼼监管政策⾛向的不明朗。在本⽉的中期
选举中，对数据中⼼建设实施限制的提案获得了美国选⺠的⼴泛⽀持，但其实际影响⽬前仍不
明朗。
花旗研究在选举后发布的报告中指出，尽管预计更严格的监管将给数据中⼼⾏业带来更多波
动，但⼤范围限制建设的可能性仍然较低。
星座能源（ Constellation Energy ）⾸席执⾏官 Joseph Dominguez 表⽰，选举后的市场反应是

“ 过度反应 ” ，并强调⺠主党⼈ “ ⾮常在意 ” 保护数据中⼼的蓝领就业岗位。
德意志银⾏的 Ryan 则认为，即便部分地区出台禁令，也只会将建设需求转移⾄监管更为宽松的
地区。
“ 在与对冲基⾦和家族办公室的讨论中， ‘ ⼀切都是成⻓股 ’ 这种论调⽆处不在 —— 这当然是荒谬
的。 ”
“ 即便对新数据中⼼的建设实施暂停令，那些已经开⼯的项⽬也必须完成。 ”
“ 归根结底，如果市场对这些设施存在需求，它们终将在某个地⽅建成。 ”

Document 328: Double Feature_ The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love

Source PDF: all report pdf/Double Feature_ The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Double Feature: The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love
Date:    8 hours ago
Double Feature: The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Double Feature: The Spy Who
Loved Me & To China With Love
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 12 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Vows on the Move / Unsplash

I. Jensen Versus The AI Trade
Charlie Munger famously warned that there are three ways smart men can
go broke: "Liquor, ladies, and leverage." NVIDIA co-founder and now
former CEO Jensen Huang has run into trouble with the second of
Munger's three L's. He won't go broke, but he is out of a good job.
On Thursday, NVIDIA announced that Huang had resigned as CEO after
33 years at the helm of the company he co-founded in 1993. His

### Page 2

departure followed revelations of extramarital affairs, including a reported
relationship with a Chinese businesswoman suspected by US intelligence
officials of spying for Beijing. Her Singapore-based company, Megaspeed,
allegedly purchased NVIDIA's high-powered GPU chips and re-exported
them to China, violating US export restrictions. The longtime CFO Colette
Kress was appointed interim CEO while the board searches for a
successor.
Huang's departure rattled the AI trade, with NVIDIA shares plunging 4.3%,

sending its forward P/E to 11.7 (charts). We promised you buying
opportunities after the midterm elections. There were a bunch last
Thursday and a few more today.

### Page 3

Competitors AMD and Broadcom advanced 5.9% and 5.5%, respectively,
on expectations of market-share gains (chart)
As a result, the Nasdaq Composite held up relatively well, falling just 0.4%
today (chart).
Neoclouds also tumbled, with CoreWeave down 6.7% and Nebius down
16.8%, amid concerns about NVIDIA-backed financing (chart). Nebius
was additionally pressured by disappointing revenue guidance and rising
capital spending.

### Page 4

Despite the latest AI jitters, the two-year Treasury yield rose 15bps to
4.87% this evening, remaining well above the federal funds rate and
signaling that further Fed tightening is warranted (chart). The 10-year
Treasury yield is back above 5.00% at 5.13% this evening.
Yields rose on higher oil prices, with Brent crude up 3.1% to $95.66 this
evening, after President Donald Trump dismissed the need for an Iran
deal, and on hawkish Fed commentary following October's hot CPI report

(chart).

### Page 5

Markets now price a 100% probability of a December Fed rate hike:
84.7% for 25bps and 15.3% for 50bps. Futures imply more than 3 rate
hikes over the next 12 months (chart).
The combination of higher Treasury yields, driven by rising oil prices and
hawkish Fed commentary, and renewed AI jitters weighed on the broader
stock market. The S&P 500 fell 0.4%, its equal-weighted counterpart
declined 0.9%, and the Russell 2000 lost 1.7% (charts).

### Page 6

II. Nvidia: The Bear Case
We can assess the risks Jensen Huang's departure poses to the broader
AI trade with our familiar framework of three constraints on the AI
investment boom.
(1) Financial constraints. NVIDIA has been described by many as the
central bank of the AI trade, conducting its own version of quantitative
easing by helping finance the expansion of AI computing capacity. The
company spent $42.2 billion purchasing equity securities during the first

half of its current fiscal year, compared with just $1.2 billion a year earlier.
The fear is that Huang's departure could slow or even end these financing
activities, tightening financial conditions for an industry already facing
questions about the returns on its enormous capital expenditures.

### Page 7

(2) Political constraints. Huang has also been the AI trade's biggest
cheerleader, tirelessly promoting its economic benefits and defending key
players. During October's AI jitters, for example, he publicly defended
CoreWeave's financial health amid a selloff in its shares and bonds,
helping restore confidence. His departure could deprive the industry of its
most influential advocate and cheerleader.
Additionally, NVIDIA reportedly intervened in the Commerce Department's

investigation into Megaspeed, while Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick
reportedly resisted efforts to blacklist the company, which remains off the
Entity List. Reports also suggest the Trump administration knew about
Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy. These allegations
could invite tougher congressional oversight and undermine Trump's
national security argument for building more data centers in the US,
making the AI buildout harder to sell to the public.

(3) Physical constraints. Washington could respond by tightening chip
export controls and closing loopholes that allow Chinese companies to
access NVIDIA-powered computing capacity remotely. If so, China might
retaliate by restricting exports of rare earth minerals, some of which are
crucial for building data centers. China has already warned Washington
against using the controversy as a pretext for imposing tougher
technology restrictions.
III. Nvidia: Our Base Case

Our base case is that NVIDIA's financing strategy remains intact. Colette
Kress, NVIDIA's longtime CFO and newly appointed interim CEO, has
worked closely with Huang since 2013 and helped oversee the company's
strategic investments and financing arrangements. During NVIDIA's
August 26 earnings call, she defended these investments against
accusations of circular financing, stating, "We recognize the scale of this
support, and we know some will call it circular financing. We see it

differently." She argued that these investments would generate attractive
returns while strengthening NVIDIA's AI ecosystem, suggesting continuity
under her leadership. We don't expect political constraints to meaningfully
increase as a result of the controversy. Nor do we expect another trade
conflict with China to erupt.

### Page 8

Even if some of the constraints intensify, they would likely turn existing
speed bumps into bigger speed bumps rather than roadblocks to the AI
investment boom. AI remains the latest evolution in the Digital Revolution
that began with IBM's mainframes, and we expect the investment boom to
continue without Huang (chart). We therefore view any resulting selloff as
another buying opportunity for the AI trade.

Document 339: European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3 according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever-volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2-0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues. As anticipated by our Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2). 
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%). According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2), driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export growth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports (-1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q). Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, growth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026, 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate (AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheless 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI-related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).

### Page 2

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced Economies Team, France  
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

Lucie Barette 
Economist, Germany, Italy and Spain 
lucie.barette@bnpparibas.com

Anis Bensaidani 
Economist, United States, Japan 
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

Document 345: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

### Page 1

TensorWave 将在美国为 OpenAI 提供 457MW 的 IT 算⼒容量，为期六年，全部搭载 AMD
Instinct MI455X GPU ，预计 2027 年上半年开始投⼊使⽤。 TensorWave 由 AMD Ventures
⽀持，今年 6 ⽉完成 B 轮融资，旨在加速 AMD 算⼒部署。
摘要：
AI 云服务商 TensorWave 与 OpenAI 达成⼀项重⼤基础设施协议，进⼀步推动 AMD 在前沿 AI 算⼒
市场的布局。
根据协议， TensorWave 将在美国多个数据中⼼站点为 OpenAI 提供 457MW （兆⽡）的 IT 算⼒容
量，为期六年，全部搭载 AMD Instinct MI455X GPU ，预计 2027 年上半年开始投⼊使⽤， 2028
年初完成全⾯部署。
这是 TensorWave 迄今规模最⼤的单笔部署，也标志着其算⼒交付能⼒从现有的⼤型集群跃升⾄
数百兆⽡量级。
此次合作是 OpenAI 与 AMD 此前宣布的战略合作伙伴关系的组成部分，旨在⽀持 OpenAI 不断增

⻓的产品与服务需求。
对市场⽽⾔，该协议意味着 AMD 在 AI 基础设施领域正加速向英伟达主导的市场格局发起挑战，
TensorWave 则成为⾸家公开宣布作为 OpenAI 与 AMD 合作基础设施提供商的新兴云服务商。
截⾄发稿， AMD 周⼀盘中下跌 5.18% 。
协议核⼼： 457 兆⽡专属算⼒，基于 AMD Helios 机架系统
根据协议条款， TensorWave 提供的 457 兆⽡ IT 容量将以专属单租⼾形式运营，基础设施专为
AMD Helios 机架级系统定制设计，并配备⾼性能以太⽹横向扩展⽹络，以⽀持⼤规模 AI ⼯作负
载。

AMD AI 训练集群之⼀，为承接下⼀代⼯作负载的算⼒需求奠定了规模基础。

市场的战略地位。

、水利设施

、市政设施

、新型基
础设施
 REITs
 和商业不动产
 REITs
。

园区基础设施
 REITs
月度行情  
截至
 2026
 年
 10
 月
 31
 日，园区基础设施
 REITs
 合计有基金
 20
 只，总市值为
258.13
亿元，流通市值为
 172.18
亿元，流通市值占比为
 66.70%
 ；涨跌情况方面，博
时津开产园
 REIT
 本月涨幅最大（
 +3.84%
 ），中金联东科创
 REIT
 本月跌幅最大（
 -
11.12%
 ）；交易数据方面，中信建投沈阳国际软件园
REIT
 月平均换手率最高（
1.04%
），
东吴苏园产业
 REIT
 月平均换手率最低（
 0.16%
）。板块层面，园区基础设施
REITs
 指
数本月下跌
 3.57%
，在
 8
个板块中排名第
 8
，板块内
 5
只基金实现上涨。  
10
月园区基础设施
 REITs
 板块
延续回调，但跌幅较
 9
月有所收窄，板块排名仍
居第
 8
位。
板块内部来看
，博时津开产园
 REIT
 、广发成都高投产业园
 REIT
、建信中
关村
 REIT
 等实现上涨，中金联东科创
 REIT
 、易方达广开产园
 REIT
 、中金亦庄产业
园
 REIT
 等调整幅度较大，板块内部走势仍有明显分化。交易活跃度方面，中信建投
沈阳国际软件园
 REIT
 月度换手继续处于板块较高水平，但较
 9
月有所下降；东吴苏
园产业
 REIT
 换手率仍处低位，资金参与热度尚未出现整体性回升。整体来看，本月
跌幅收窄或更多体现为前期调整后的局部修复，短期市场表现仍可能受到市场情绪、
流动性环境及基金估值消化进度等多重因素的综合影响。中长期来看，园区资产的结
构性分化格局尚未改变；未来城市更新项目与
 REITs
 发行范围的有机衔接，或将为板
块提供新的发展动能。其中，运营体系成熟、租约结构稳定且区位条件较优的项目有
望
持续受益
。

 月度换手处于板块较高
水平，中金印力消费
 REIT
 换手率仍处低位。整体来看，指数跌幅收窄但上涨基金数
量有所减少。中长期来看，扩大内需和促消费
 等相关
政策有望为线下消费场景提供需
求支撑，购物中心等资产的经营表现有望逐步改善。具备区位优势、成熟运营体系和
持续业态调整能力的项目，更有望保持经营韧性。

储备及存量资产盘活提供支持。区位条件较好、出租率稳定且运营管理成熟的项目，

三、
重要事件及市场动态  
重要事件  
1
、
公募基金运作规则拟修订，
 REITs
 投资比例适用口径进一步
明确 
10
月
 9
日，中国证监会就《公开募集证券投资基金运作管理办法（征求意见稿）》
及配套规则公开征求意见。征求意见稿将不动产投资信托基金纳入基金类别，并拟明
确基金财产投资
 REITs
 时适用证券投资相关比例要求。相关安排有助于厘清公募基金
投资
 REITs
 的规则口径，为公募资金参与
 REITs
 市场提供制度基础。目前文件仍处
于征求意见阶段，具体影响需待正式规则落地后进一步观察。  
2
、
证监会部署加快推进
 REITs
纳入沪深港通，市场开放预期进
一步增强  
10
月
 19
日，中国证监会主席吴清在
 2026
 金融街论坛年会上发表主题演讲，提
出
"
落实内地与香港市场
 '
五个协同
 '
合作新模式，加快推进人民币股票交易柜台和
REITs
 纳入沪深港通
 "
。相关部署为内地与香港
REITs
 市场互联互通提供了政策方向。
若后续落地，有望拓宽
 REITs
 投资者范围，提升跨境配置便利度，并对市场流动性和
定价效率形成积极影响。  
3
、
全国首单水利设施发电
 REITs
上市，水利资产证券化实现突
破 
10
月
 28
日，银华粤海水务水利
 REIT
 在深交所上市。该产品底层资产为飞来峡
水利枢纽、潮州供水枢纽发电相关特定资产及发电业务收费权，是全国首单水利设施
发电
 REITs
、全国首单省级水利
 REITs
，也是广东首单水利
 REITs
。募集资金拟用于
黄茅峡水库工程、环北部湾广东水资源配置工程等重大水利工程建设。该项目将存量
水利资产盘活与新增工程投资相衔接，为社会资本参与水利基础设施建设提供了新的
渠道，也拓展了公募
 REITs
 在水利领域的应用。

四、
核心观点  
10
月，中证
REITs
（收盘）指数下跌
0.84%
 ，中证
REITs
 全收益指数下跌
 0.37%
。
REITs
 市场整体延续调整态势，但表现仍优于主要股指。成交方面，成交规模环比与
同比均维持回落趋势，市场活跃度整体表现相对平淡。一级市场新品集中上市与二级

### Page 16

中航证券
研究所
发布 
市场成交缩量并存，构成本月较为突出的运行特征
 ，供给端扩容尚未同步转化
 为存量
品种交易活跃度的提升。  
从内部结构
来
看，
10
月板块
表现较上月有所切换
。保障性租赁住房、仓储物流和
生态环保
 REITs
 表现相对靠前，其中保障性租赁住房延续稳健表现，仓储物流由
 前期
调整转为修复；园区基础设施和新型基础设施
 REITs
 回调幅度相对较大
，各类型
板块
间
轮动持续性有限。个基上涨面有所扩大，但不同板块及同类资产内部仍存在较为明
显的分化。  
一级市场方面，
本
月共有
 5
只
 REITs
 上市
，
覆盖保障性租赁住房、水利设施、仓
储物流和商业不动产四类资产。与此前月份单月新增产品相对较少的情况相比，本月
上市节奏有所加快
，
且新增基金并非集中于单一板块，市场扩容呈现多类资产共同推
进的特征。两只保障性租赁住房
 REITs
 先后上市，使住房保障领域成为本月扩容较为
集中的方向，也为市场增加了以租赁运营为基础的收益型资产。银华粤海水务水利
REIT
 上市，将水利设施发电相关资产纳入公募
 REITs
 市场，进一步拓展了水利资产
的证券化范围，并为盘活存量水利资产、支持新增水利投资提供了实践样本。中金菜
鸟
 REIT
 上市，为仓储物流板块补充了新的物流节点资产。华安锦江商业
 REIT
 则将
商业不动产
 REITs
 的底层业态延伸至酒店，为观察酒店资产的经营现金流和估值定价
提供了新的市场样本。整体
 来
看，
REITs
 市场的持续扩容已不再仅体现为基金数量增
加，而是逐步体现为底层资产、现金流来源、区域覆盖和原始权益人类型的同步丰富
 ，
进一步
提升
 REITs
 市场对不同类型存量资产的承接能力
 。 
政策层面
，制度推进有望与供给扩容形成
 协同
。
10
月
 9
日，
证监会就公募基金运
作管理办法及配套规则公开征求意见，拟进一步明确公募基金投资
 REITs
 的相关规则
口径。同时，监管部门
于
 10
月
 19
日
提出加快推进
 REITs
 纳入沪深港通，为内地与香
港市场互联互通
明确
了政策方向。
如若政策逐步落地，
REITs
 市场容量
有望逐步
扩大
，
市场
可投资标的丰富度有望进一步提升
 。 
投资策略方面，建议继续以现金流稳定性和资产质量作为筛选基础，并结合市场
扩容后的资产谱系加强分类比较。
 近期新品上市数量较多，
 对于新上市基金，应重点
考察底层资产质量、发行估值、运营安排和现金流
 情况
；对于存量基金，则应持续跟
踪经营
与
分派兑现情况，在控制估值与流动性风险的基础上，适度关注基本面预期改
善带来的配置机会。长期来看，可重点关注以下方向：  
1.
交通基础设施
 REITs
 具有一定的垄断性和刚需属性，具备较强的稳定性。板块
内部因区域经济与产业结构差异呈现分化，部分路产车流量增长承压，而核心城市群
路产表现相对稳健，
 10
 月板块整体小幅调整，但内部表现并非一致走弱，区域位置、
路网结构和运营差异仍是
 个基
定价的重要依据。中长期看，交通强国建设和收费公路
相关制度完善，有助于为优质路产提供相对稳定的发展环境。建议优先关注具有区域
垄断地位、路网位置较为核心、车流量和通行费收入可预期性较强的项目。

### Page 17

中航证券
研究所
发布 
2.
仓储物流
 REITs
 板块
近期
有所修复。随着新品供给增加，区位条件、租户结构
和租约安排的重要性将更加突出
 ，同时，
 "
十五五
"
规划将现代流通体系和产业链供应
链安全作为重点，预计将有更多一线及新一线城市的枢纽节点项目入市，为市场注入
优质增量。建议重点关注位于核心物流节点、租户结构相对分散、续租能力较强且现
金流稳定的产品。  
3.
消费基础设施
 REITs
。
消费基础设施的收益表现取决于客流恢复
 、
客流转化、
租户组合、租金调整和主动运营能力。体验式业态占比高、运营调改能力强的购物中
心有望率先兑现盈利改善，估值
 存在
修复空间。后续在扩大内需和消费设施建设相关
政策支持下，具备成熟运营体系、业态调整能力和稳定品牌合作资源的商业项目更有
可能形成持续经营优势。  
4.
新型基础设施
 REITs
。
"
东数西算
"
、
"
数字经济
"
国家战略与
 AI
算力需求扩张共
振，数据中心、智算中心兼具政策红利、高成长性与稀缺赛道属性
 ，且行业
发展为数
据中心等资产提供
了
中长期需求基础。
考虑到
顶层设计已将数据中心纳入
 REITs
 资产
范围，
板块估值中枢有望维持在相对较高水平
 。建议优先关注合同质量较高、客户信
用较好、运营稳定性较强的项目
 。 
5.
园区基础设施
 REITs
。板块经过持续调整后，部分项目的估值压力有所释放。
结构上，厂房类资产依托制造业基本盘，或持续受益于先进制造业扩产与产业链自主
可控趋势；
 "
十五五
"
时期老旧街区、厂区改造任务明确，城市更新项目纳入
 REITs
 发
行范围后，园区存量资产的盘活路径进一步打通。具备优质区位、成熟运营能力与均
衡业态组合的园区资产，其估值修复空间值得重点关注。

五、
风险提示  
流动性不足风险
 ：部分
 REITs
 规模较小、投资者关注度低，可能存在二级市场交易
不活跃的情况。  
政策调控风险
：
REITs
 受行业政策影响较大，土地政策、税收政策、行业监管政策
等政策变化可能对
 REITs
 收益产生影响。

### Page 3

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

4
Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 10

10
Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?
dbDataInsights survey – September 2026, based on overseeing development while managing cyber risk

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015-2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

95% Confidence Interval Breach impact negative at 95% confidence Cumulative impact of cyberincident

### Page 14

Marks & Spencer (Reported Apr 2025)
What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd
party forced the suspension of all online
orders and led to empty store shelves.
What did it cost?
~£136mn of direct costs related to the
attack, estimated group operating profit
impact of around £300mn for the FY. M&S
also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.
Coupang (Reported Nov 2025)
What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.
Equifax (Reported Sep 2017)
What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

### Page 15

15
Notable cyber attacks: how they happened, estimated costs and share price reaction, cont.
Nike (Reported Jan 2026)
What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product
specifications and pricing), following expiry
of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating
the incident, it did not report any
operational disruption. Underperformance

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.
Jaguar Land Rover (Reported Sep 2025)
What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.

### Page 16

16
Targets go beyond tech and can also impact critical infrastructure
MGM Resorts (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
A vishing call to the IT help desk was reportedly used to reset credentials, after which
ransomware took down hotel systems, bookings and casino floor operations for several
days.
What did it cost?
MGM declined to pay a ransom. Around $100mn hit to third-quarter 2023 results plus
roughly ~$10mn of additional one-time expenses related to the incident.

Caesars Entertainment (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
The same social-engineering group as in the MGM incident targeted an outsourced IT
support vendor, gaining access to the loyalty programme database and member data.
What did it cost?
Caesars paid roughly $15mn of a reported $30mn demand and kept customer-facing
operations running.
NHS / Synnovis (Reported Jun 2024)
What happened?
Ransomware against pathology provider Synnovis significantly reduced its capacity to

process tests leading to delays / cancellations of over 11,000 appointments.
What did it cost?
Estimated 2024 direct operational financial impact of around GBP 32mn for Synnovis,
several times the profit reported in the prior year, plus wider NHS disruption costs.
Colonial Pipeline (Reported May 2021)
What happened?
A compromised VPN credential without multi-factor authentication allowed attackers to
access the network where they subsequently deployed ransomware. The operator shut the

pipeline down, triggering fuel shortages (c. 70% of gas stations in some states ran out of
fuel) across the US East Coast.
What did it cost?
A $4.4mn ransom was paid, of which about $2.3mn was later recovered by the US
authorities.
Bitget (Reported Sep 2026)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a vulnerability in a 3rd party security product to steal internal network
credentials, forge withdrawal instructions, bypass risk controls and ultimately steal crypto.

What did it cost?
An estimated $388mn worth of funds was transferred out by the attackers. While the firm
reimbursed affected users via its User Protection Fund, there were significant outflows
following the incident as users moved to take funds off the platform.
Progress Software (Reported May 2023)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a zero-day SQL injection vulnerability in Progress Software’s MOVEit file
transfer system. This was then used by attackers to steal data from firms running the

Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026

Cyber breaches can impact consumer behaviour
Roughly 4 in 10 consumers we surveyed say they would stop buying from a company they shop with after a known breach or attack.
Source: dbDataInsights monthly consumer survey, Deutsche Bank Research. EU Average includes France, Germany, Italy and Spain.
How will customers respond to a data breach once it occurs?
dbDataInsights survey of consumer responses, % of respondents
How will customers react once the breach is resolved?

Consumer responses among those who would either stop buying or were unsure, % of respondents
Consumer surveys suggest that cyber breaches can undermine customer
loyalty.  Results of our dbDataInsights Consumer survey show the potential
mindset of a consumer after a cyber breach occurs:
—Across the US, UK and EU average, 39–41% say they would stop buying, while only
27–28% would continue. Around a third are unsure.
—Among those who would stop buying or are unsure, only 18–22% say they would

return as normal. Another 30–35% would buy selected products only, 17–20%
would not return, and 28–29% remain unsure. Note that this implies a range of
13-15% believe they would not shop at the store again after the breach is
resolved.
Admittedly, these stated intentions may overstate actual switching, particularly for
essential purchases where alternatives are limited. Nevertheless, the findings
suggest that cyber breaches that put customer privacy at risk can influence

purchasing decisions, especially if they have alternatives.
